        Idea:
Clean up the structural smells in the video-editor resize/preview architecture that have caused recurring bugs all session. The most recent symptom: when a user drags an interior boundary inside a pinned shot group, the live preview position is subtly wrong during the drag (boundary lags or jitters), but the committed position on release is correct. The root cause is structural — the live preview math and the commit math live in two different code paths that recompute the same answer in different files, the dual state (resizePreviewRef vs resizeOverrides) lets the rendered position drift one frame behind the synchronous source of truth, and the cursor-to-handle offset isnt captured anywhere so the boundary always trails the cursor by some pixels. None of these are bugs — theyre places where the architecture relies on parallel implementations agreeing, with no shared function or test enforcing it.

This is a focused structural cleanup, not a re-architecture. Five items, in priority order by leverage. Each item is testable in isolation.

State of completion BEFORE this megaplan: Coming out of the previous finish projection rollback megaplan, the data shape is fully soft-tag, the resize session model is unified (clip | group-child-boundary | group-edge), useClipDrag/useClipResize/usePinnedGroupSync/useSwitchToFinalVideo/useExternalDrop/useTimelineTrackManagement are all rewritten for the soft-tag model, the deprecated shims are gone, and 210/210 video-editor tests pass. The user has confirmed dragging an interior boundary ends up perfect on release — so the commit path is correct. The bug is purely in the live preview being subtly off from the committed result.

The 5 cleanups:

1. Extract computeResizeBoundary as a shared pure function (highest leverage). Currently TimelineCanvas.updateResize (the live preview) and useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd (the commit) both compute the new boundary position given a cursor delta — but in different files, with different intermediate variable names, and no shared function. Create src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts with computeGroupChildBoundaryResize and computeGroupEdgeResize pure functions. Both live preview and commit call these. Add unit tests for clamping, direction, min-duration.

2. Capture the cursor-to-handle offset at session start. The resize handle is 8px wide. deltaSeconds = (clientX - initialClientX) / pixelsPerSecond assumes the cursor STARTED ON the boundary. Fix: at pointer-down, compute boundaryPixel = startLeft + (dir === left ? action.start : action.end) * pixelsPerSecond and cursorOffsetPx = clientX - boundaryPixel. Store on session. In updateResize: cursorBoundaryPixel = clientX - cursorOffsetPx; cursorBoundaryTime = (cursorBoundaryPixel - startLeft) / pixelsPerSecond. Apply for all three resize kinds.

3. Eliminate the dual resizePreviewRef / resizeOverrides state. Use useSyncExternalStore to subscribe to the ref, or a version counter. Eliminate the React state lag. SortableRow renders from the same source the commit reads.

4. Centralize pixel ↔ time conversion. Extract useTimelineScale({ scale, scaleWidth, startLeft }) returning pixelsPerSecond, pixelsPerSecondRef, timeToPixel, pixelToTime. All consumers use it.

5. Add an end-to-end resize integration test using @testing-library/react + fireEvent. Render real TimelineCanvas with shot group of 3 clips. Fire pointerdown on middle clip right handle, pointermove, assert DOM boundary position matches expected. Fire pointerup, assert no jump. Variant for group-edge.

Hard requirements: User symptom fixed. No regressions in: drag clip body in pinned group, drag right edge of last clip, drag left edge of first clip, drag interior boundary, cross-track drag grouped clip, switch shot to/from final video, drag shot from gallery, auto-save defers, poll-sync defers, migration loads projection-shape. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/. All video-editor tests pass (210 currently). Shared computeGroupChildBoundaryResize/computeGroupEdgeResize exist with unit tests and both live preview AND commit call them. Cursor offset captured for all 3 resize kinds. Dual state collapsed to one source. useTimelineScale exists and used by all consumers. At least one end-to-end resize test.

Constraints: video-editor tool only, no DB schema changes, follow CLAUDE.md, prefer Edit over Write, dont bundle unrelated cleanups, leave pre-existing non-video-editor failures alone, no new state management libraries, new resize-math functions PURE (no hooks/refs/side effects), e2e test uses @testing-library/react + fireEvent.

Success: boundary tracks cursor pixel-perfectly during drag, tsc clean, all tests pass, new pure functions referenced by both live preview and commit (grep-verifiable), dual state collapsed (grep verifiable), at least one e2e pointer test passes, resize subsystem line count decreases net.

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata. - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 2.4 against `materializedata()` in `usetimelinecommit` and `assembletimelinedata()` in `timeline-data.ts`. the plan still says to overwrite `pinnedshotgroups` after `materializedata()` returns, but `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so mutating the config afterward would leave signature-derived state stale unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Clean up the structural smells in the video-editor resize/preview architecture that have caused recurring bugs all session. The most recent symptom: when a user drags an interior boundary inside a pinned shot group, the live preview position is subtly wrong during the drag (boundary lags or jitters), but the committed position on release is correct. The root cause is structural — the live preview math and the commit math live in two different code paths that recompute the same answer in different files, the dual state (resizePreviewRef vs resizeOverrides) lets the rendered position drift one frame behind the synchronous source of truth, and the cursor-to-handle offset isnt captured anywhere so the boundary always trails the cursor by some pixels. None of these are bugs — theyre places where the architecture relies on parallel implementations agreeing, with no shared function or test enforcing it.

This is a focused structural cleanup, not a re-architecture. Five items, in priority order by leverage. Each item is testable in isolation.

State of completion BEFORE this megaplan: Coming out of the previous finish projection rollback megaplan, the data shape is fully soft-tag, the resize session model is unified (clip | group-child-boundary | group-edge), useClipDrag/useClipResize/usePinnedGroupSync/useSwitchToFinalVideo/useExternalDrop/useTimelineTrackManagement are all rewritten for the soft-tag model, the deprecated shims are gone, and 210/210 video-editor tests pass. The user has confirmed dragging an interior boundary ends up perfect on release — so the commit path is correct. The bug is purely in the live preview being subtly off from the committed result.

The 5 cleanups:

1. Extract computeResizeBoundary as a shared pure function (highest leverage). Currently TimelineCanvas.updateResize (the live preview) and useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd (the commit) both compute the new boundary position given a cursor delta — but in different files, with different intermediate variable names, and no shared function. Create src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts with computeGroupChildBoundaryResize and computeGroupEdgeResize pure functions. Both live preview and commit call these. Add unit tests for clamping, direction, min-duration.

2. Capture the cursor-to-handle offset at session start. The resize handle is 8px wide. deltaSeconds = (clientX - initialClientX) / pixelsPerSecond assumes the cursor STARTED ON the boundary. Fix: at pointer-down, compute boundaryPixel = startLeft + (dir === left ? action.start : action.end) * pixelsPerSecond and cursorOffsetPx = clientX - boundaryPixel. Store on session. In updateResize: cursorBoundaryPixel = clientX - cursorOffsetPx; cursorBoundaryTime = (cursorBoundaryPixel - startLeft) / pixelsPerSecond. Apply for all three resize kinds.

3. Eliminate the dual resizePreviewRef / resizeOverrides state. Use useSyncExternalStore to subscribe to the ref, or a version counter. Eliminate the React state lag. SortableRow renders from the same source the commit reads.

4. Centralize pixel ↔ time conversion. Extract useTimelineScale({ scale, scaleWidth, startLeft }) returning pixelsPerSecond, pixelsPerSecondRef, timeToPixel, pixelToTime. All consumers use it.

5. Add an end-to-end resize integration test using @testing-library/react + fireEvent. Render real TimelineCanvas with shot group of 3 clips. Fire pointerdown on middle clip right handle, pointermove, assert DOM boundary position matches expected. Fire pointerup, assert no jump. Variant for group-edge.

Hard requirements: User symptom fixed. No regressions in: drag clip body in pinned group, drag right edge of last clip, drag left edge of first clip, drag interior boundary, cross-track drag grouped clip, switch shot to/from final video, drag shot from gallery, auto-save defers, poll-sync defers, migration loads projection-shape. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/. All video-editor tests pass (210 currently). Shared computeGroupChildBoundaryResize/computeGroupEdgeResize exist with unit tests and both live preview AND commit call them. Cursor offset captured for all 3 resize kinds. Dual state collapsed to one source. useTimelineScale exists and used by all consumers. At least one end-to-end resize test.

Constraints: video-editor tool only, no DB schema changes, follow CLAUDE.md, prefer Edit over Write, dont bundle unrelated cleanups, leave pre-existing non-video-editor failures alone, no new state management libraries, new resize-math functions PURE (no hooks/refs/side effects), e2e test uses @testing-library/react + fireEvent.

Success: boundary tracks cursor pixel-perfectly during drag, tsc clean, all tests pass, new pure functions referenced by both live preview and commit (grep-verifiable), dual state collapsed (grep verifiable), at least one e2e pointer test passes, resize subsystem line count decreases net.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata.         Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Clean up the structural smells in the video-editor resize/preview architecture that have caused recurring bugs all session. The most recent symptom: when a user drags an interior boundary inside a pinned shot group, the live preview position is subtly wrong during the drag (boundary lags or jitters), but the committed position on release is correct. The root cause is structural — the live preview math and the commit math live in two different code paths that recompute the same answer in different files, the dual state (resizePreviewRef vs resizeOverrides) lets the rendered position drift one frame behind the synchronous source of truth, and the cursor-to-handle offset isnt captured anywhere so the boundary always trails the cursor by some pixels. None of these are bugs — theyre places where the architecture relies on parallel implementations agreeing, with no shared function or test enforcing it.

This is a focused structural cleanup, not a re-architecture. Five items, in priority order by leverage. Each item is testable in isolation.

State of completion BEFORE this megaplan: Coming out of the previous finish projection rollback megaplan, the data shape is fully soft-tag, the resize session model is unified (clip | group-child-boundary | group-edge), useClipDrag/useClipResize/usePinnedGroupSync/useSwitchToFinalVideo/useExternalDrop/useTimelineTrackManagement are all rewritten for the soft-tag model, the deprecated shims are gone, and 210/210 video-editor tests pass. The user has confirmed dragging an interior boundary ends up perfect on release — so the commit path is correct. The bug is purely in the live preview being subtly off from the committed result.

The 5 cleanups:

1. Extract computeResizeBoundary as a shared pure function (highest leverage). Currently TimelineCanvas.updateResize (the live preview) and useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd (the commit) both compute the new boundary position given a cursor delta — but in different files, with different intermediate variable names, and no shared function. Create src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts with computeGroupChildBoundaryResize and computeGroupEdgeResize pure functions. Both live preview and commit call these. Add unit tests for clamping, direction, min-duration.

2. Capture the cursor-to-handle offset at session start. The resize handle is 8px wide. deltaSeconds = (clientX - initialClientX) / pixelsPerSecond assumes the cursor STARTED ON the boundary. Fix: at pointer-down, compute boundaryPixel = startLeft + (dir === left ? action.start : action.end) * pixelsPerSecond and cursorOffsetPx = clientX - boundaryPixel. Store on session. In updateResize: cursorBoundaryPixel = clientX - cursorOffsetPx; cursorBoundaryTime = (cursorBoundaryPixel - startLeft) / pixelsPerSecond. Apply for all three resize kinds.

3. Eliminate the dual resizePreviewRef / resizeOverrides state. Use useSyncExternalStore to subscribe to the ref, or a version counter. Eliminate the React state lag. SortableRow renders from the same source the commit reads.

4. Centralize pixel ↔ time conversion. Extract useTimelineScale({ scale, scaleWidth, startLeft }) returning pixelsPerSecond, pixelsPerSecondRef, timeToPixel, pixelToTime. All consumers use it.

5. Add an end-to-end resize integration test using @testing-library/react + fireEvent. Render real TimelineCanvas with shot group of 3 clips. Fire pointerdown on middle clip right handle, pointermove, assert DOM boundary position matches expected. Fire pointerup, assert no jump. Variant for group-edge.

Hard requirements: User symptom fixed. No regressions in: drag clip body in pinned group, drag right edge of last clip, drag left edge of first clip, drag interior boundary, cross-track drag grouped clip, switch shot to/from final video, drag shot from gallery, auto-save defers, poll-sync defers, migration loads projection-shape. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/. All video-editor tests pass (210 currently). Shared computeGroupChildBoundaryResize/computeGroupEdgeResize exist with unit tests and both live preview AND commit call them. Cursor offset captured for all 3 resize kinds. Dual state collapsed to one source. useTimelineScale exists and used by all consumers. At least one end-to-end resize test.

Constraints: video-editor tool only, no DB schema changes, follow CLAUDE.md, prefer Edit over Write, dont bundle unrelated cleanups, leave pre-existing non-video-editor failures alone, no new state management libraries, new resize-math functions PURE (no hooks/refs/side effects), e2e test uses @testing-library/react + fireEvent.

Success: boundary tracks cursor pixel-perfectly during drag, tsc clean, all tests pass, new pure functions referenced by both live preview and commit (grep-verifiable), dual state collapsed (grep verifiable), at least one e2e pointer test passes, resize subsystem line count decreases net.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 2.4 against `materializedata()` in `usetimelinecommit` and `assembletimelinedata()` in `timeline-data.ts`. the plan still says to overwrite `pinnedshotgroups` after `materializedata()` returns, but `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so mutating the config afterward would leave signature-derived state stale unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Clean up the structural smells in the video-editor resize/preview architecture that have caused recurring bugs all session. The most recent symptom: when a user drags an interior boundary inside a pinned shot group, the live preview position is subtly wrong during the drag (boundary lags or jitters), but the committed position on release is correct. The root cause is structural — the live preview math and the commit math live in two different code paths that recompute the same answer in different files, the dual state (resizePreviewRef vs resizeOverrides) lets the rendered position drift one frame behind the synchronous source of truth, and the cursor-to-handle offset isnt captured anywhere so the boundary always trails the cursor by some pixels. None of these are bugs — theyre places where the architecture relies on parallel implementations agreeing, with no shared function or test enforcing it.

This is a focused structural cleanup, not a re-architecture. Five items, in priority order by leverage. Each item is testable in isolation.

State of completion BEFORE this megaplan: Coming out of the previous finish projection rollback megaplan, the data shape is fully soft-tag, the resize session model is unified (clip | group-child-boundary | group-edge), useClipDrag/useClipResize/usePinnedGroupSync/useSwitchToFinalVideo/useExternalDrop/useTimelineTrackManagement are all rewritten for the soft-tag model, the deprecated shims are gone, and 210/210 video-editor tests pass. The user has confirmed dragging an interior boundary ends up perfect on release — so the commit path is correct. The bug is purely in the live preview being subtly off from the committed result.

The 5 cleanups:

1. Extract computeResizeBoundary as a shared pure function (highest leverage). Currently TimelineCanvas.updateResize (the live preview) and useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd (the commit) both compute the new boundary position given a cursor delta — but in different files, with different intermediate variable names, and no shared function. Create src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts with computeGroupChildBoundaryResize and computeGroupEdgeResize pure functions. Both live preview and commit call these. Add unit tests for clamping, direction, min-duration.

2. Capture the cursor-to-handle offset at session start. The resize handle is 8px wide. deltaSeconds = (clientX - initialClientX) / pixelsPerSecond assumes the cursor STARTED ON the boundary. Fix: at pointer-down, compute boundaryPixel = startLeft + (dir === left ? action.start : action.end) * pixelsPerSecond and cursorOffsetPx = clientX - boundaryPixel. Store on session. In updateResize: cursorBoundaryPixel = clientX - cursorOffsetPx; cursorBoundaryTime = (cursorBoundaryPixel - startLeft) / pixelsPerSecond. Apply for all three resize kinds.

3. Eliminate the dual resizePreviewRef / resizeOverrides state. Use useSyncExternalStore to subscribe to the ref, or a version counter. Eliminate the React state lag. SortableRow renders from the same source the commit reads.

4. Centralize pixel ↔ time conversion. Extract useTimelineScale({ scale, scaleWidth, startLeft }) returning pixelsPerSecond, pixelsPerSecondRef, timeToPixel, pixelToTime. All consumers use it.

5. Add an end-to-end resize integration test using @testing-library/react + fireEvent. Render real TimelineCanvas with shot group of 3 clips. Fire pointerdown on middle clip right handle, pointermove, assert DOM boundary position matches expected. Fire pointerup, assert no jump. Variant for group-edge.

Hard requirements: User symptom fixed. No regressions in: drag clip body in pinned group, drag right edge of last clip, drag left edge of first clip, drag interior boundary, cross-track drag grouped clip, switch shot to/from final video, drag shot from gallery, auto-save defers, poll-sync defers, migration loads projection-shape. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/. All video-editor tests pass (210 currently). Shared computeGroupChildBoundaryResize/computeGroupEdgeResize exist with unit tests and both live preview AND commit call them. Cursor offset captured for all 3 resize kinds. Dual state collapsed to one source. useTimelineScale exists and used by all consumers. At least one end-to-end resize test.

Constraints: video-editor tool only, no DB schema changes, follow CLAUDE.md, prefer Edit over Write, dont bundle unrelated cleanups, leave pre-existing non-video-editor failures alone, no new state management libraries, new resize-math functions PURE (no hooks/refs/side effects), e2e test uses @testing-library/react + fireEvent.

Success: boundary tracks cursor pixel-perfectly during drag, tsc clean, all tests pass, new pure functions referenced by both live preview and commit (grep-verifiable), dual state collapsed (grep verifiable), at least one e2e pointer test passes, resize subsystem line count decreases net.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 2.4 against `materializedata()` in `usetimelinecommit` and `assembletimelinedata()` in `timeline-data.ts`. the plan still says to overwrite `pinnedshotgroups` after `materializedata()` returns, but `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so mutating the config afterward would leave signature-derived state stale unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the grouped-drag plan against `useclipdrag` and `usetimelinetrackmanagement.movecliptorow()`. the plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still does not say how `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` is updated when a grouped drag or move lands on a different row, so the overlay and menu state can still point at the old track after a successful cross-track move. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the cross-track multi-drag new-track branch in `useclipdrag`. that path still commits through `applyedit({ type: 'config', resolvedconfig })` using `buildconfigfromdragresult()`, and the revised plan does not explain how pinned-group metadata is updated atomically on that path, so grouped drags that create a new track are still under-specified even beyond the stale-`trackid` problem. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed shared interaction-state location against the actual hook graph in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:194`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.ts:172-204`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.contexts.ts:72-166`. rev 3 says the ref should live in `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts`, but that file only memoizes context values while `useclipdrag`, `timelinecanvas`, and `usepinnedgroupsync` are all coordinated from `timelineeditor`, so the stated location and rationale are still technically off.         Project directory:

        Idea:
Clean up the structural smells in the video-editor resize/preview architecture that have caused recurring bugs all session. The most recent symptom: when a user drags an interior boundary inside a pinned shot group, the live preview position is subtly wrong during the drag (boundary lags or jitters), but the committed position on release is correct. The root cause is structural — the live preview math and the commit math live in two different code paths that recompute the same answer in different files, the dual state (resizePreviewRef vs resizeOverrides) lets the rendered position drift one frame behind the synchronous source of truth, and the cursor-to-handle offset isnt captured anywhere so the boundary always trails the cursor by some pixels. None of these are bugs — theyre places where the architecture relies on parallel implementations agreeing, with no shared function or test enforcing it.

This is a focused structural cleanup, not a re-architecture. Five items, in priority order by leverage. Each item is testable in isolation.

State of completion BEFORE this megaplan: Coming out of the previous finish projection rollback megaplan, the data shape is fully soft-tag, the resize session model is unified (clip | group-child-boundary | group-edge), useClipDrag/useClipResize/usePinnedGroupSync/useSwitchToFinalVideo/useExternalDrop/useTimelineTrackManagement are all rewritten for the soft-tag model, the deprecated shims are gone, and 210/210 video-editor tests pass. The user has confirmed dragging an interior boundary ends up perfect on release — so the commit path is correct. The bug is purely in the live preview being subtly off from the committed result.

The 5 cleanups:

1. Extract computeResizeBoundary as a shared pure function (highest leverage). Currently TimelineCanvas.updateResize (the live preview) and useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd (the commit) both compute the new boundary position given a cursor delta — but in different files, with different intermediate variable names, and no shared function. Create src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts with computeGroupChildBoundaryResize and computeGroupEdgeResize pure functions. Both live preview and commit call these. Add unit tests for clamping, direction, min-duration.

2. Capture the cursor-to-handle offset at session start. The resize handle is 8px wide. deltaSeconds = (clientX - initialClientX) / pixelsPerSecond assumes the cursor STARTED ON the boundary. Fix: at pointer-down, compute boundaryPixel = startLeft + (dir === left ? action.start : action.end) * pixelsPerSecond and cursorOffsetPx = clientX - boundaryPixel. Store on session. In updateResize: cursorBoundaryPixel = clientX - cursorOffsetPx; cursorBoundaryTime = (cursorBoundaryPixel - startLeft) / pixelsPerSecond. Apply for all three resize kinds.

3. Eliminate the dual resizePreviewRef / resizeOverrides state. Use useSyncExternalStore to subscribe to the ref, or a version counter. Eliminate the React state lag. SortableRow renders from the same source the commit reads.

4. Centralize pixel ↔ time conversion. Extract useTimelineScale({ scale, scaleWidth, startLeft }) returning pixelsPerSecond, pixelsPerSecondRef, timeToPixel, pixelToTime. All consumers use it.

5. Add an end-to-end resize integration test using @testing-library/react + fireEvent. Render real TimelineCanvas with shot group of 3 clips. Fire pointerdown on middle clip right handle, pointermove, assert DOM boundary position matches expected. Fire pointerup, assert no jump. Variant for group-edge.

Hard requirements: User symptom fixed. No regressions in: drag clip body in pinned group, drag right edge of last clip, drag left edge of first clip, drag interior boundary, cross-track drag grouped clip, switch shot to/from final video, drag shot from gallery, auto-save defers, poll-sync defers, migration loads projection-shape. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/. All video-editor tests pass (210 currently). Shared computeGroupChildBoundaryResize/computeGroupEdgeResize exist with unit tests and both live preview AND commit call them. Cursor offset captured for all 3 resize kinds. Dual state collapsed to one source. useTimelineScale exists and used by all consumers. At least one end-to-end resize test.

Constraints: video-editor tool only, no DB schema changes, follow CLAUDE.md, prefer Edit over Write, dont bundle unrelated cleanups, leave pre-existing non-video-editor failures alone, no new state management libraries, new resize-math functions PURE (no hooks/refs/side effects), e2e test uses @testing-library/react + fireEvent.

Success: boundary tracks cursor pixel-perfectly during drag, tsc clean, all tests pass, new pure functions referenced by both live preview and commit (grep-verifiable), dual state collapsed (grep verifiable), at least one e2e pointer test passes, resize subsystem line count decreases net.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Clean up the structural smells in the video-editor resize/preview architecture that have caused recurring bugs all session. The most recent symptom: when a user drags an interior boundary inside a pinned shot group, the live preview position is subtly wrong during the drag (boundary lags or jitters), but the committed position on release is correct. The root cause is structural — the live preview math and the commit math live in two different code paths that recompute the same answer in different files, the dual state (resizePreviewRef vs resizeOverrides) lets the rendered position drift one frame behind the synchronous source of truth, and the cursor-to-handle offset isnt captured anywhere so the boundary always trails the cursor by some pixels. None of these are bugs — theyre places where the architecture relies on parallel implementations agreeing, with no shared function or test enforcing it.

This is a focused structural cleanup, not a re-architecture. Five items, in priority order by leverage. Each item is testable in isolation.

State of completion BEFORE this megaplan: Coming out of the previous finish projection rollback megaplan, the data shape is fully soft-tag, the resize session model is unified (clip | group-child-boundary | group-edge), useClipDrag/useClipResize/usePinnedGroupSync/useSwitchToFinalVideo/useExternalDrop/useTimelineTrackManagement are all rewritten for the soft-tag model, the deprecated shims are gone, and 210/210 video-editor tests pass. The user has confirmed dragging an interior boundary ends up perfect on release — so the commit path is correct. The bug is purely in the live preview being subtly off from the committed result.

The 5 cleanups:

1. Extract computeResizeBoundary as a shared pure function (highest leverage). Currently TimelineCanvas.updateResize (the live preview) and useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd (the commit) both compute the new boundary position given a cursor delta — but in different files, with different intermediate variable names, and no shared function. Create src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts with computeGroupChildBoundaryResize and computeGroupEdgeResize pure functions. Both live preview and commit call these. Add unit tests for clamping, direction, min-duration.

2. Capture the cursor-to-handle offset at session start. The resize handle is 8px wide. deltaSeconds = (clientX - initialClientX) / pixelsPerSecond assumes the cursor STARTED ON the boundary. Fix: at pointer-down, compute boundaryPixel = startLeft + (dir === left ? action.start : action.end) * pixelsPerSecond and cursorOffsetPx = clientX - boundaryPixel. Store on session. In updateResize: cursorBoundaryPixel = clientX - cursorOffsetPx; cursorBoundaryTime = (cursorBoundaryPixel - startLeft) / pixelsPerSecond. Apply for all three resize kinds.

3. Eliminate the dual resizePreviewRef / resizeOverrides state. Use useSyncExternalStore to subscribe to the ref, or a version counter. Eliminate the React state lag. SortableRow renders from the same source the commit reads.

4. Centralize pixel ↔ time conversion. Extract useTimelineScale({ scale, scaleWidth, startLeft }) returning pixelsPerSecond, pixelsPerSecondRef, timeToPixel, pixelToTime. All consumers use it.

5. Add an end-to-end resize integration test using @testing-library/react + fireEvent. Render real TimelineCanvas with shot group of 3 clips. Fire pointerdown on middle clip right handle, pointermove, assert DOM boundary position matches expected. Fire pointerup, assert no jump. Variant for group-edge.

Hard requirements: User symptom fixed. No regressions in: drag clip body in pinned group, drag right edge of last clip, drag left edge of first clip, drag interior boundary, cross-track drag grouped clip, switch shot to/from final video, drag shot from gallery, auto-save defers, poll-sync defers, migration loads projection-shape. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/. All video-editor tests pass (210 currently). Shared computeGroupChildBoundaryResize/computeGroupEdgeResize exist with unit tests and both live preview AND commit call them. Cursor offset captured for all 3 resize kinds. Dual state collapsed to one source. useTimelineScale exists and used by all consumers. At least one end-to-end resize test.

Constraints: video-editor tool only, no DB schema changes, follow CLAUDE.md, prefer Edit over Write, dont bundle unrelated cleanups, leave pre-existing non-video-editor failures alone, no new state management libraries, new resize-math functions PURE (no hooks/refs/side effects), e2e test uses @testing-library/react + fireEvent.

Success: boundary tracks cursor pixel-perfectly during drag, tsc clean, all tests pass, new pure functions referenced by both live preview and commit (grep-verifiable), dual state collapsed (grep verifiable), at least one e2e pointer test passes, resize subsystem line count decreases net.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata.
